OBJECTIVES & DURATION
OBJECTIVES
Deploy & Maintain the OmniSwitch switches in an access network
Deploy & Maintain OmniAccess Stellar WLAN access points
Use the OmniVista Cirrus solution to configure & Supervize the OmniSwitch switches and the OmniAccess 
Stellar access points
DURATION & METHODS
• Duration: 2 days
• Methods
- Live Sessions >  Theoritical Presentations
- Asynchronous Sessions > Tutored labs, done from a Remote-Labs (R-Labs)

SECURITY
IEC62443-3-3 Level 2 Ready in 8.10R3
The IEC 62443 standard, which is focused on the security of industrial automation and control systems (IACS)
This enhance allows and administrator to enforce a password refresh                                                          
for a specific user or all users upon their next login.
user password-refresh
user <string> password-refresh 
This enhancement provides the ability to convert certificates in DER, PEM, PKCS#12, and P7B to PEM format.
aaa certificate convert-cert
This enhance provides the ability to check a certificate's revocation status using either CRL (Certificate 
Revocation List) or OCSP (Online Certificate Status Protocol). Currently supported for Radius and Syslog over 
TLS.
ssl pki check-revocation

request

information

request
Success /

exit-on-fail Configures if the switch must authenticate using all servers in the list or only the first available 
server. When enabled, the switch uses only the first available server in the list to check for user information. 
When disabled, the switch uses all the available servers in the list to check for user information. 
aaa authentication {console | telnet | ftp | http | snmp | ssh | default} server1 [server2...] [local] [exit-on-fail {enable | disable}]

ip interface master emp address 172.25.167.203 mask 255.255.255.224
OS6860N
USB Ethernet Dongle (8.9.R1)
• This feature allows for a USB-to-Ethernet interface for switches that 
lack an EMP port. This interface is treated just like an EMP interface. 
• All functions and CLIs related to EMP are applicable to the USB-to-
Ethernet dongle.
Notes:
• USB 3.0 version dongles are supported on OS6360/6465/6560 models. 
• USB 2.0 version dongles are supported on all models.
• All the chassis of a VC should have a USB-to-Ethernet dongle for proper VC EMP functionality.

<<<PAGE 75>>>
EASY CONFIGURATION WITH LIGHTNING CONFIGURATION
• The easy configuration process (Lightning configuration) starts if:
• Only first or second physical port connected with the client, no other ports connected
• No prior switch configuration exist
• No DHCP address assignment occurs after boot up
• No remote configuration load (RCL) server and OmniVista NMS connection exists
• Connected device should have an IP address assigned to its Ethernet connection (manual setup) or 
DHCP client configuration the the switch will assign an IP to the client 192.168.0.200 
• AOS will allow local authentication and enables HTTPS access to WebView ONLY via physical port 
#1/1-2 (on VLAN 1).
• Once switch configuration is saved (i.e. write memory), the default IP address is no longer 
available (i.e. will be internally removed)

<<<PAGE 80>>>
3 
Lab: Connection & Use of the Stellar Remote Lab 
 
 2 
Discover the Remote Lab Topology 
In this part, you will find an explanation about the Remote Lab topology that you are going to use during this 
training.  
2.1. 
Topology of the Stellan Remote Lab Pod 
 
 
This topology contains: clients, Stellar Access points, OmniSwitches, servers and an OmniVista Cirrus. 
It simulates a three-tier end user topology (Access, Aggregation and Core), as well as the clients, servers and 
internet connection to your OmniVista Cirrus. 
2.2. 
Stellar Access Points 
You will manage and configure the Stellar Access Points AP1301 and AP1321 through the OmniVista Cirrus. 
You will configure the network, so they can communicate with the DHCP server and send traffic to the 
OmniVista Cirrus through the internet link. 
2.3. 
Access Layer and Clients 
 
You will manage and configure the wired (Client 05) and wireless client (Raspberry Pi “Wifi client”). 
You will manage and configure both Access OmniSwitches (OS-6360 and OS-2360). 
All the traffic will be switched (L2 switching) at this level. And DHCP, DNS, HTTP,… requests will be 
forwarded to the aggregation switch, then to the servers in our core network.

<<<PAGE 81>>>
4 
Lab: Connection & Use of the Stellar Remote Lab 
 
2.4. 
Aggregation Layer 
 
You will manage and configure parts of the aggregation OmniSwitch OS6870. 
But, DO NOT MODIFY OR DELETE the default configuration loaded on the OS-6870 by the reset script 
(explained in the next lab). 
All the layer 3 routing protocols will be preconfigured by the script. And some Layer 2 VLANs can be 
preconfigured. 
Keep this default configuration and build on top of it as explained during the labs of this training. 
The OS6870 implements features such as DHCP relay and dynamic routing. This allows your Clients and Stellar 
Access Points to communicate with the servers and OmniVista Cirrus. 
2.5. 
Core Layer 
 
DO NOT MANAGE AND CONFIGURE the core switch OS6900. Its job, with his default configuration pushed by 
our reset script, is to route (OSPF, RIP) the traffic between our three OmniSwitches and our servers. 
This OS6900 will be transparent during your training, this is why you don’t have a shortcut to reach it. 
2.6. 
Servers 
 
You must have the DHCP server (“AAA Training Server POD##”) and NAT/Firewall server (“Pod##_pfSense”) 
started and running to perform the labs. 
But, DO NOT MANAGE AND CONFIGURE these servers. They are preconfigured with all the scopes, routes,.. 
needed. 
The Stellar Access Points and all the clients who will be authenticated will get their IP address from this 
DHCP server. 
And all the outgoing traffic (to OmniVista Cirrus and internet) have to go through the NAT and Firewall VM.

training.

Modifying the OmniSwitch 6360 Configuration 
2.1. 
Enabling the Stellar AP1321 port on the OmniSwitch SW5-6360A  
- In the default configuration loaded on the OmniSwitch 6360, the user ports are disabled. First, enable 
the port 1/1/6 (where is connected the Stellar AP1321): 
 
OS6360-XTE210 -> interfaces 1/1/6 admin-state enable 
2.2. 
Enabling the LAN Client VM port on the OmniSwitch SW5-6360A

Configure the OmniSwitch 6360 IP interface > 192.168.1.2  
 
- Create an IP interface on the OmniSwitch 6360 to reach the AP Stellar 1321: 
 
OS6360-XTE210 -> ip interface int_1 address 192.168.1.2/24 vlan 1

OmniSwitch Switches - Remote Access 
 
 1 
Accessing the OmniSwitch switch Remotely 
In this lab, we are going to authorize the remote connection to the switch by using the SSH protocol, then the 
HTTP protocol. To do so, the IP EMP interface will be used.  
 2 
Authenticating to the OmniSwitch switch  
Authenticated Switch Access (ASA) provides the ability to restrict which users can configure the switch 
remotely. Switch login attempts can be challenged via the local database, or a remote database such as RADIUS 
or LDAP. ASA applies to Telnet, FTP, SNMP, SSH, HTTP, and the console and modem ports. 
2.1. 
Enabling the SSH connection 
- Log into the OS6360, then use the command to verify that the switch is checking its local database when 
an SSH connection is attempted:  
 
OS6360-XTE210-> show aaa authentication 
Service type = Default 
  1st authentication server  = local 
  Authentication exit-on-fail: Enabled 
Service type = Console 
  1st authentication server  = local 
  Authentication exit-on-fail: Enabled 
Service type = Telnet 
  Authentication = Use Default, 
  1st authentication server  = local 
  Authentication exit-on-fail: Enabled 
Service type = Ftp 
  Authentication = Use Default, 
  1st authentication server  = local 
  Authentication exit-on-fail: Enabled 
Service type = Http 
  Authentication = Use Default, 
  1st authentication server  = local 
  Authentication exit-on-fail: Enabled 
Service type = Snmp 
  Authentication = Use Default, 
  1st authentication server  = local 
  Authentication exit-on-fail: Enabled 
Service type = Ssh 
  Authentication = Use Default, 
  1st authentication server  = local 
  Authentication exit-on-fail: Enabled 
 
 
Notes > Why “local”? 
The keywork “local” in “1st authentication server = local” means that the local database will be the first 
database to be polled for authentication information. 
 
- By default, the SSH protocol is denied on the OS6360. To allow it, enter the following command: 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> aaa authentication ssh local

<<<PAGE 108>>>
3 
OmniSwitch Switches - Remote Access

- Insert the EMP IP address (“Host”) > 10.4.X.5 (X = R-Lab Number[25-32], check EMP IP with command 
show ip interface on 6360) 
 
 
 
 
- Click on the Continue button: 
 
 
 
- Fill the credentials (admin/switch): 
 
 
 
- You are now connected to the OS6360 via SSH:

OmniSwitch Switches - Remote Access 
 
 3 
Connect to the WebView 
The OmniSwitch can also be monitored and configured by using the WebView (Alcatel-Lucent Enterprise’s web-
based device management tool). The WebView application is embedded in the OmniSwitch and is accessible via 
a web browser. 
3.1. 
Setting up the HTTP Session  
- Check that the HTTP service is enabled: 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> show aaa authentication 
--- 
Service type = Http 
  Authentication = Use Default, 
  1rst authentication server = denied 
  Authentication exit-on-fail: Enabled 
--- 
 
- As you can see here, the http authentication is denied.  
- To enable the HTTP authentication, and define the first authentication server to be polled to be the local 
database: 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> aaa authentication http local 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> show aaa authentication 
--- 
Service type = Http 
  Authentication = Use Default, 
  1rst authentication server = local 
  Authentication exit-on-fail: Enabled 
--- 
 
- Check the WebView status:  
 
OS6360-XTE210-> show webview 
WebView Server = Enabled, 
WebView Access = Enabled, 
WebView Force-SSL = Enabled, 
WebView HTTPS-Port = 443, 
WebView SSL-Strong-Ciphers = Enabled 
Webview TLS Version = 1.2

<<<PAGE 110>>>
5 
OmniSwitch Switches - Remote Access 
 
3.2. 
Opening the WebView 
- From the access virtual machine’s Windows Desktop, open a web browser (ex. Firefox, Chrome) 
- In the URL bar, type https://10.4.X.5 (X = R-Lab Number[25-32])  
 
 
 
- Login to the WebView with the admin credentials: 
 
User Name : admin 
Password : switch 
Language : English 
 
- After a successful connection, the dashboard page appears. 
 
- The switch configuration is divided into seven main configuration groups: 
o 
Physical,  
o 
Layer 2,  
o 
Networking 
o 
Service management, 
o 
Security 
o 
Quality of service 
o 
Device management.

<<<PAGE 111>>>
6 
OmniSwitch Switches - Remote Access 
 
3.3. 
Configuring the OmniSwitch from the WebView 
- First, we are going to change the Inactivity Timer from the WebView. 
- From the horizontal menu bar at the top of the page, select Security > ASA, then click Session and then 
Configuration in the left-hand side menu. 
 
- Change the value to 45 for the CLI interface and 15 for the WebView, then click on Apply at the bottom 
of the page:  
 
 
 
- From the CLI, check that the modification has been applied: 
Open the OS6360 console terminal. Login with the credential admin/switch. 
Enter the following command to check that the settings have been applied: 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> show session config 
Cli Default Prompt               = OS6360 ->, 
Cli Banner File Name             = , 
Cli Inactivity Timer in minutes  = 45, 
Ftp Banner File Name             = , 
Ftp Inactivity Timer in minutes  = 4, 
Http Inactivity Timer in minutes = 15, 
Http Banner File Name            = , 
Login Timer in seconds           = 55, 
Maximum number of Login Attempts = 3, 
 
- Go back to the WebView application. In the horizontal icon bar at the top of the page, select the third 
icon from the left (write memory). 
 
  
 
Click yes to save the modification in the active directory (running).

CONFIGURATION BACKUP & RESTORE
• Configuration Backup
• Backup of the session banner, userTable* and vcboot.cfg files
• The configuration backup command creates a .tar file where are stored the collected files 
• The tar file name is “configuration_backup.tar” and will be placed in “/flash/config-backup-
recovery” folder
• Up to 10 .tar files can be stored in the /flash/config-backup-recovery directory
• Configuration Restore
• When the “restore” option is used, the switch: 
• Selects the “configuration_backup.tar” file in “/flash/config-backup-recovery” folder 
• Extract the .tar file to get the userTable, session banner, and vcboot.cfg files. 
R8

<<<PAGE 126>>>
AOS MANAGING FILES/DIRECTORIES 
• USB Backup and Restore
• If a USB drive is plugged in, switch will store image files, power supply and system configuration 
files to USB storage drive automatically upon user commands “write memory” or “copy running-
certified” “copy flash-synchro” if USB backup is enabled on switch. 
• The USB drive can be used to restore images and config (power supply and system) from the USB 
drive on a switch with usb auto-copy command enabled.
• If the user configures a password at the time of enabling the back-up and restore, then the 
corresponding back-up and restore content will be encrypted and decrypted.
R8
usb backup admin-state {enable | disable}  [key <> | hash-key<>]
usb auto-copy <enable | disable> copy-config <enable| disable> from <directory-path> 
[key <> | hash-key<>]

<<<PAGE 127>>>
THIN CLIENT OMNISWITCH 
• No configuration is stored on the switch. It will contact OmniVista 2500 to retrieve the 
config.
• Thin-client mode is configured through the activation process. 
• Switch boots up normally and registers to OV 2500 as part of the activation process. 
• Thin-client mode must be configured as part of the activation response message.
• In thin-client mode, no configuration is saved in the ‘running’ directory
• But there will be vcboot.cfg with the minimal network reachability configuration.
• ‘write memory’ can be executed but configurations will not be saved to the vcboot,cfg file. 
• All configuration changes should be done in OV 2500.
OmniVista 2500
Callhome
Sends Config

OmniSwitches Directories Content (R8) 
 
 1 
Introduction 
In Release 8, the management of an OmniSwitch is controlled by 2 types of files: 
- 
Images files, which are proprietary code developed by Alcatel-Lucent Enterprise to run the hardware.  
- 
A configuration files, named vcboot.cfg and vcsetup.cfg, in text format, sets and controls the 
configurable functions.  
 
The directory structure that store the image and configuration files is divided in several parts:  
- 
The certified directory contains files that have been certified by an authorized user as the default files 
for the switch.  
- 
The working directory is a holding place for new files. Files in the working directory must be tested 
before committing them to the certified directory.  
- 
The user-defined directories are created by the user and are like the working directory in that they can 
contain image and configuration files.  
 
- 
The running directory is the directory where the configuration changes will be saved.  
- 
The running configuration, stored in the RAM, contains the current operating parameters of the 
OmniSwitch obtained from the image and configuration files.  
 2 
Viewing the Image & Configuration Files 
- 
Logging into the OmniSwitch 
o 
Open the OS6360 serial console (shortcut available on the Windows desktop). 
o 
Use following authentication credentials: 
Login: admin 
Password: switch 
 3 
Checking the working and certified Directories 
3.1. 
Displaying the working and certified directories content 
- 
Check the files that are in each directory by entering the following: 
OS63630-XTE210 -> ls -l /flash/working |or| ls -l /flash/certified    
total 98636 
-rw-r--r--    1 admin    user     100973976 Mar  4 17:33 Nosa.img 
-rw-------    1 root     root            47 Jul 15 07:07 boot.md5 
-rw-r--r--    1 admin    user           153 Apr 26 13:51 cloudagent.cfg 
-rw-r--r--    1 admin    user            75 May 20  2021 imgsha256sum 
-rw-r--r--    1 admin    user           155 Mar  4 17:37 naasclient.cfg 
drwxr-xr-x    5 admin    user           504 May 17  2021 pkg 
-rw-r--r--    1 admin    user           382 May 20  2021 software.lsm 
-rw-r--r--    1 admin    user          2138 Jul 15 07:17 vcboot.cfg 
-rw-r--r--    1 admin    user           331 Jul 15 07:17 vcsetup.cfg

<<<PAGE 132>>>
3 
OmniSwitches Directories Content (R8) 
 
3.2. 
Displaying the microcode version 
- 
To display the microcode version installed on the OmniSwitch: 
OS63630-XTE210 -> show microcode working |or| show microcode certified |or| show microcode loaded 
   /flash/working 
   Package           Release                 Size     Description 
-----------------+-------------------------+---------+----------------------------------- 
Nosa.img           8.8.152.R01                111683640 Alcatel-Lucent OS 
 
 
Notes: “Loaded”? 
- Loaded displays the currently active microcode versions.  
- Entering the command show microcode also displays the currently active microcode version.  
 4 
Booting behavior in Release 8 
 
- 
At the time of a normal boot (cold start):  
- 
The switch will reboot from certified directory if contents (images and vcboot.cfg) are different from 
the running directory (which can be the working directory, or a user-defined directory).  
- 
If contents are the same, the switch will reboot from the running directory (which can be the working 
directory, or a user-defined directory). 
 
 
Warning > The “reload all” command particularity 
IF THE OMNISWITCH IS REBOOTED WITH THE “RELOAD ALL” COMMAND, IT WILL REBOOT FROM THE CERTIFIED 
DIRECTORY, NO MATTER WHAT THE CONTENT OF THE RUNNING DIRECTORY IS (SAME/DIFFERENT THAN THE 
CERTIFIED DIRECTORY CONTENT)  
 
- If the running directory is the certified directory, you will not be able to save any changes made to the 
running directory. If the switch reboots, any configuration changes will be lost. In order to save 
configuration changes, the running directory cannot be the certified directory. 
 5 
Determining from which directory the switch was loaded? 
When a switch boots the RUNNING CONFIGURATION will come from either the certified, working, or 
a user-defined directory. A switch can be rebooted to run from any directory using the reload from command. 
 
To check from which directory the OmniSwitch is running, and the content comparison between the WORKING 
and CERTIFIED directories:  
OS63630-XTE210 -> show running-directory 
 
CONFIGURATION STATUS 
  Running CMM              : MASTER-PRIMARY, 
  CMM Mode                 : VIRTUAL-CHASSIS MONO CMM, 
  Current CMM Slot         : CHASSIS-1 A, 
  Running configuration    : WORKING, 
  Certify/Restore Status   : CERTIFIED 
SYNCHRONIZATION STATUS 
  Running Configuration    : SYNCHRONIZED 
 
 
- 
Running configuration: WORKING > the OmniSwitch is running from the working directory.  
- 
Certify/Restore Status: CERTIFIED > the working directory content matches the certified directory 
content.

<<<PAGE 133>>>
4 
OmniSwitches Directories Content (R8) 
 
- 
Running Configuration: SYNCHRONIZED > the running configuration matches the WORKING configuration. 
 6 
Synchronizing RAM and Running Directory 
Perform some configuration to make the running configuration different from the configuration stored in the 
working and certified directories. Observe what happens.  
 
- 
Performing modifications in the configuration 
o 
Create 3 new VLANs (2, 3, and 99): 
 
OS63630-XTE210 -> show vlan 
 vlan    type   admin   oper    ip    mtu          name 
------+-------+-------+------+------+------+------------------ 
1      std       Ena     Dis   Dis    1500    VLAN 1 
4094   vcm       Ena     Dis   Dis    1500    VCM IPC 
 
 
OS63630-XTE210 -> vlan 2 
OS63630-XTE210 -> vlan 3 
OS63630-XTE210 -> vlan 99 
 
OS63630-XTE210 -> show vlan 
 vlan    type   admin   oper    ip    mtu          name 
------+-------+-------+------+------+------+------------------ 
[…] 
2      std       Ena     Dis   Dis    1500    VLAN 2 
3      std       Ena     Dis   Dis    1500    VLAN 3 
99     std       Ena     Dis   Dis    1500    VLAN 99 
[…] 
 
- 
3 new VLANs are now created. Changes are made to the configuration file in RAM. These changes take 
effect immediately but are not written permanently; they will be lost if the OmniSwitch reboots.  
 
OS63630-XTE210 -> show running-directory 
 
CONFIGURATION STATUS 
  Running CMM              : MASTER-PRIMARY, 
  CMM Mode                 : VIRTUAL-CHASSIS MONO CMM, 
  Current CMM Slot         : CHASSIS-1 A, 
  Running configuration    : WORKING, 
  Certify/Restore Status   : CERTIFIED 
SYNCHRONIZATION STATUS 
  Running Configuration    : NOT SYNCHRONIZED 
 
- 
Running configuration: WORKING > the OmniSwitch is running from the WORKING directory.  
- 
Certify/Restore Status: CERTIFIED > the working directory content matches the certified directory 
content. 
- 
Running Configuration: NOT SYNCHRONIZED > the running configuration does not match the 
configuration of the working directory. 
 
 
Warning > What if the OmniSwitch reboots now? 
IF THE OMNISWITCH IS REBOOTED NOW (VIA A COMMAND RELOAD FROM WORKING … OR IF POWER TO THE 
OMNISWITCH IS INTERRUPTED), THE OMNISWITCH WILL BOOT, ALL THE CHANGES IN THE RUNNING 
CONFIGURATION WILL BE OVERWRITTEN, AND THE OMNISWITCH WILL ROLL BACK TO THE WORKING DIRECTORY, 
SINCE THE WORKING AND CERTIFIED DIRECTORIES ARE THE SAME.  
 
IN OUR CASE, THE VLAN 2, 3 AND 99 WILL BE LOST, AS THEY ARE NOW STORED IN THE RUNNING 
CONFIGURATION.

<<<PAGE 134>>>
5 
OmniSwitches Directories Content (R8) 
 
 7 
Saving the Running Configuration to Working Directory 
Save the configuration (VLANs created previously) from the running directory to the working directory. Verify it 
by using CLI commands.  
 
- 
To save the running configuration to the working directory: 
OS63630-XTE210 -> write memory 
 
File /flash/working/vcsetup.cfg replaced. 
 
File /flash/working/vcboot.cfg replaced. 
 
 
- 
To check that:  
OS63630-XTE210 -> show running-directory 
 
CONFIGURATION STATUS 
  Running CMM              : MASTER-PRIMARY, 
  CMM Mode                 : VIRTUAL-CHASSIS MONO CMM, 
  Current CMM Slot         : CHASSIS-1 A, 
  Running configuration    : WORKING, 
  Certify/Restore Status   : CERTIFY NEEDED 
SYNCHRONIZATION STATUS 
  Running Configuration    : SYNCHRONIZED 
 
- 
Running configuration: WORKING > the OmniSwitch is running from the working directory.  
- 
Certify/Restore Status: CERTIFY NEEDED > the WORKING directory does not match the CERTIFIED 
directory. 
- 
Running Configuration: SYNCHRONIZED > the running configuration matches the configuration of the 
working directory. 
 
 
 
 
Warning > What if the OmniSwitch reboots now? 
IF THE OMNISWITCH IS REBOOTED NOW (VIA A COMMAND RELOAD ALL OR IF POWER TO THE OMNISWITCH IS 
INTERRUPTED), THE OMNISWITCH WILL BOOT FROM THE CERTIFIED DIRECTORY, ALL THE CHANGES IN THE 
RUNNING CONFIGURATION WILL BE OVERWRITTEN, AND THE OMNISWITCH WILL ROLL BACK TO THE CERTIFIED 
DIRECTORY.  
 
HOWEVER, SINCE THE CONFIGURATION FILE WAS SAVED TO THE WORKING DIRECTORY, THAT FILE IS STILL IN 
THE WORKING DIRECTORY AND CAN BE RETRIEVED.  
 
SINCE THE WORKING AND CERTIFIED DIRECTORIES ARE NOT THE SAME, THE OMNISWITCH WILL BE RUNNING 
FROM THE CERTIFIED DIRECTORY.  
 
- 
Let’s reboot the OmniSwitch and see what happens: 
OS63630-XTE210 -> reload all 
Only one reload may be active in VC mode, other scheduled reloads will be canceled 
Confirm Reload All (Y/N) : y 
 
This operation will verify and copy images before reloading. 
It may take several minutes to complete.

 8 
Creating a User-Defined Directory 
User-defined directories are like the working directory in that they can contain image and configuration 
files. These directories can have any name and can be used to store additional switch configurations. 
Configuration changes CAN be saved directly to any user-defined directory. 
 
 
- 
Create a user defined directory and copy the contents of the WORKING directory to it: 
 
OS63630-XTE210 -> mkdir lab 
OS63630-XTE210 -> cp working/*.* lab 
cp: can't open 'working/boot.md5': Permission denied 
 
 
Tips 
The lab directory may have been already created, ignore error and proceed on. 
During the copy; it tries to copy the boot.md5 file but a “permission denied” message is displayed. This file is 
auto generated so ignore this error and proceed. 
- 
Now let’s see what files are stored in the newly created directory: 
OS63630-XTE210 -> ls lab 
Nosa.img         naasclient.cfg   vcboot.cfg 
cloudagent.cfg   software.lsm     vcsetup.cfg 
 
- 
Boot the switch from the new user-defined directory (lab): 
OS63630-XTE210 -> reload from lab no rollback-timeout 
Confirm Activate (Y/N): y 
 
- 
Once the switch boots, verify that it booted from the lab directory:  
OS63630-XTE210 -> show running-directory 
 
CONFIGURATION STATUS 
  Running CMM              : MASTER-PRIMARY, 
  CMM Mode                 : VIRTUAL-CHASSIS MONO CMM, 
  Current CMM Slot         : CHASSIS-1 A, 
  Running configuration    : lab, 
  Certify/Restore Status   : CERTIFY NEEDED 
SYNCHRONIZATION STATUS 
  Running Configuration    : SYNCHRONIZED 
 
- 
Running configuration: lab > the OmniSwitch is running from the user-defined lab.  
- 
Certify/Restore Status: CERTIFY NEEDED > the running directory (“lab”) does not match the CERTIFIED 
directory.  
- 
Running Configuration: SYNCHRONIZED > the running configuration matches the configuration stored in 
the running directory (here the user-defined “lab” directory) 
 
 
Warning > What if the OmniSwitch reboots now? 
IF THE OMNISWITCH IS REBOOTED (IF THE POWER TO THE OMNISWITCH IS INTERRUPTED), THE OMNISWITCH 
WILL BOOT FROM THE CERTIFIED DIRECTORY, SINCE THE RUNNING (LAB) AND CERTIFIED DIRECTORIES ARE NOT 
THE SAME (Certify/Restore Status: CERTIFY NEEDED).  
 
- 
Overwrite the contents of the certified directory with the configuration from the running directory 
(“lab” directory here):  
OS63630-XTE210 -> copy running certified 
Wed Apr  2 04:22:40 : flashManager FlashMgr Main INFO message: 
+++ Verifying image directory lab on CMM flash 
Wed Apr  2 04:23:04 : ChassisSupervisor MipMgr INFO message: 
+++ Copy running to certified succeeded

Notes  
The copy running certified command should only be done if the running configuration has been verified. 
 
- Check the synchronization status: 
OS63630-XTE210 -> show running-directory 
CONFIGURATION STATUS 
  Running CMM              : MASTER-PRIMARY, 
  CMM Mode                 : VIRTUAL-CHASSIS MONO CMM,

SYNCHRONIZATION STATUS 
  Running Configuration    : SYNCHRONIZED 
 
- 
Running configuration: lab > the OmniSwitch is running from the user-defined lab.  
- 
Certify/Restore Status: CERTIFIED > the running directory (“lab”) matches the CERTIFIED directory.  
- 
Running Configuration: SYNCHRONIZED > the running configuration matches the configuration stored in 
the running directory (here the user-defined “lab” directory) 
 
Warning > What if the OmniSwitch reboots now? 
IF THE OMNISWITCH IS REBOOTED (IF THE POWER TO THE OMNISWITCH IS INTERRUPTED), THE OMNISWITCH 
WILL BOOT FROM THE “LAB” DIRECTORY, SINCE THE RUNNING (LAB) AND CERTIFIED DIRECTORIES ARE THE SAME 
(Certify/Restore Status: CERTIFIED).  
 9 
Changing the User Directory 
Another useful command can be used in Release 8: the modify running-directory command. This command 
allows to change the running directory and allow configuration changes to be saved to the new running 
directory. Useful if start on certified and want to switch to working or user directory to be able to save 
configuration 
- 
The running directory is currently “lab”. Let’s change it back to “working”:  
 
OS63630-XTE210 -> show running-directory

In Release 8, it is also possible to backup the images and configuration from certified and running directories 
into a USB key (/uflash/6360/certified and /uflash/6360/running directories).

<<<PAGE 144>>>
POE POWER MANAGEMENT
• Fast PoE : 2X60, 6360, 6860E, 6860N, 6865, 6870
• Note: OS6360 – P10A does not support FPoE
• Used to provide PoE power a few seconds after powering up the chassis 
• Allows the chassis to immediately provide PoE power to any connected device after powering up 
without waiting for the chassis to finish booting
• Fast PoE requires an upgraded FPGA/CPLD, refer to the release note. 
-> lanpower fpoe {enable | disable}

<<<PAGE 145>>>
POE POWER MANAGEMENT
• Perpetual PoE : 2X60, 6360, 6860E, 6860N, 6865, 6870
• Note: OS6360 – P10A does not support PPoE.
• Provides uninterrupted power to the connected device (PD) even when the switch is restarting or 
recharging, such as during a soft restart
• Perpetual PoE requires an upgraded FPGA/CPLD, refer to the release note
-> lanpower ppoe {enable | disable}

<<<PAGE 146>>>
ENERGY EFFICIENT ETHERNET (EEE) – IEEE 802.3AZ 
STANDARD
• Protocol to allow chipset to go to a low power 
mode state when idle (i.e. no traffic sent)
• Compatibles with OmniSwitches models except
« U » models (optical fiber models).
• EEE is only applicable to OmniSwitch copper 
ports operating at 100/1000 Mbps speed
• Switch compatibility refer to specifications 
guide 
EEE activé
EEE activé

<<<PAGE 147>>>
POWER OVER ETHERNET
• OmniSwitch switches with PoE capabilities can provide power to a large range of 
equipments (ex: IP phones, access points, PTZ cameras,…)
• PoE priority and configurable maximum power per port for power allocation 
• Dynamic PoE Allocation: Provide only the amount of power needed by powered devices (PD) up to 
the total energy budget for the most efficient power consumption possible

POE MANAGEMENT
• Displays the power supplies hardware information and current status:
• Setting the PoE Operational Status
• Reactivating / Deactivating power to one port
• Setting the maximum amount of inline power

Total     PS

1/1         920       AC       UP       Internal

-> lanpower slot 1/1 service start

-> lanpower port 1/1/24 power 18000

for one port (in mW)
for a slot (in W)

<<<PAGE 151>>>
POE MANAGEMENT
• Setting the PoE Operational Status on a Port
• Disabled by default
• Setting Port Priority Levels (Low, High, Critical)
• Default priority level for a port is low
• Low: In the event of a power management issue, inline power to low-priority ports is interrupted 
first 
• High: This value is used for port(s) that have important, but not mission-critical, devices attached. 
If other ports in the chassis have been configured as critical, inline power to high-priority ports is 
given second priority.
• Critical: In the event of a power management issue, inline power to critical ports is maintained as 
long as possible 
-> lanpower port 1/1/1 admin-state enable
-> lanpower port 1/1/6 priority critical

<<<PAGE 152>>>
POE MANAGEMENT
• Setting the Capacitor Detection Method
• Not compatible with IEEE specification 802.3af
• It should only be enabled to support legacy IP phones
• Setting Priority Disconnect Status
• Used by the system software in determining whether an incoming PD will be granted or denied 
power when there are too few watts remaining in the PoE power budget for an additional device
-> lanpower slot 1/1 capacitor-detection enable
-> lanpower slot 1/1 priority-disconnect enable

<<<PAGE 154>>>
POE POWER MANAGEMENT
• Delayed-feature – 6360, 6560, 6465 and 6870
• This feature is used to introduce a delay in lanpower on system bootup.This delay is used 
to start a lanpower after some specific delay to leave some time for system stability.
• To enable Delayed – start feature with specific delay value
• To disable the Delayed- start feature
• To display the delayed-start configurations
-> lanpower slot 1/1 delayed-start enable seconds <num>
->lanpower slot 1/1 delayed-start disable
->show lanpower slot <chassis/slot> / all  delayed-start
<num> - specific delay value in seconds in multiples of 5. Value should be within 120 to 600 seconds
Notes 
• Start the lanpower service before enabling the 
delay start feature.  
• It is mandatory to do write memory to reflect this 
command on bootup. 
• Lanpower service starts after the delay timer 
expiry
• User can force stop the delay timer by applying 
lanpower service stop command on boot while 
on the period of delay-timer is activated
• Fpoe and Ppoe is not supported on enabling this 
feature.
On boot while delayed lanpower timer is running, status in “Show lanpower slot </>/all status” is updated 
with “Delayed” with time

802.1Q – VLAN TAGGING

<<<PAGE 165>>>
IEEE 802.1Q – TAGGED VLANS
• Objective
• Aggregates multiple VLANs across Ethernet links
•
Combines traffic from multiple VLANs over a single link
•
Encapsulates bridged frames within standard IEEE 802.1Q frame
•
Enabled on fixed ports
•
Tags port traffic for destination VLAN
Tagged Frames

<<<PAGE 166>>>
IEEE 802.1Q – TAGGED VLANS
• VLAN Tag
• 802.3 MAC header change
• 4096 unique VLAN Tags (addresses)
• VLAN ID == GID == VLAN Tag
802.1P
Three bits field within 802.1Q header
Allows up to 8 different priorities
Feature must be implemented in hardware
802.1p (3 bits)
DA
SA
VLAN ID (12 Bits)
4 bytes
“Modified MAC 802.3”
Ethertype, Priority, Tag

VLAN – MOBILE TAG

<<<PAGE 169>>>
VLAN – MOBILE TAG
• Allows the dynamic assignment of mobile ports to more than one VLAN at the same time
• Enabled on mobile ports
-> vlan 3 mobile-tag enable
• Allows mobile ports to receive 802.1Q tagged packets 
•
Enables the classification of mobile port packets based on 802.1Q VLAN ID tag = “3”
•
Takes precedence over all VLAN Rules

mobile port traffic

Statically assigns (tags) fixed ports to one or more

PoE, VLAN & DHCP Configuration 
 
 1 
Introduction 
In this exercise, we will:  
- Check that the PoE is active on the OmniSwitch 6360.  
- Create several VLANs on the OmniSwitch 6360. We will use them in the next labs.  
- Once the PoE enabled and the VLANs created, we will configure the access point to get an IP address 
dynamically (DHCP). Reminder: In a previous lab, you have manually configured the IP address of the 
access point to 192.168.1.3.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
Notes 
In this lab, we will use the CLI to configure the PoE & VLAN features.   
It is interesting to note that this lab can also be done from the WebView (OmniSwitch administration web 
page). You can, therefore, if you wish, perform this whole lab from the WebView!  
 2 
Monitor the PoE on the OmniSwitch 6360 
The PoE (Power over Ethernet) is enabled by default on the OmniSwitch 6360. No configuration is needed.

Stellar AP1321

 3 
Creating VLANs on the OmniSwitch 6360 
3.1. 
Creating the VLANs  
 
Create the following VLANs:  
- VLAN 10 dedicated to the administration of the access point. 
- VLAN 20 dedicated to Employees. 
- VLAN 30 dedicated to Guests. 
Assign these VLANs to the ports 1/1/3 and 1/1/6. 
 
3.1.1. 
Creating the VLANs via the CLI 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 10 name Management-AP 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 20 name Employees 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 30 name Guests 
 
- Check the result:  
 
OS6360-XTE210-> show vlan 
vlan    type   admin   oper    ip    mtu          name 
------+-------+-------+------+------+------+------------------ 
[…] 
10     std       Ena     Dis   Dis    1500    Management-AP 
20     std       Ena     Dis   Dis    1500    Employees 
30     std       Ena     Dis   Dis    1500    Guests 
[…] 
3.1.2. 
Assign the VLANs to the ports 
- Assign the VLANs to the port 1/1/3 (where the backbone is connected):  
The VLAN 10, 20 and 30 as tagged VLANs. 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 10 members port 1/1/3 tagged 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 20 members port 1/1/3 tagged 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 30 members port 1/1/3 tagged 
 
- Assign the VLANs to the port 1/1/6 (where is connected the access point):  
The VLAN 10 (Administration AP) as default/untagged VLAN.  
The VLAN 20 and 30 as tagged VLANs. 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 10 members port 1/1/6 untagged 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 20 members port 1/1/6 tagged 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 30 members port 1/1/6 tagged 
 
- Check the result: 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> show vlan members port 1/1/3 
 
  vlan      type        status 
--------+-----------+---------------

    20    tagged      inactive 
    30    tagged      inactive 
 
OS6360-XTE210-> show vlan members port 1/1/6

--------+-----------+--------------- 
    10    untagged    forwarding 
    20    tagged      forwarding 
    30    tagged      forwarding

can join the DHCP server (located in the "backbone" area in the diagram below) and be assigned an IP address 
automatically.  
 
 
 
That's what we're going to do in this part.

OS6360-XTE210-> interfaces 1/1/3 admin-state enable

Procedure: Employee SSID & Guests SSID Creation 
 
 1 
Introduction 
In this lab, we will create:  
- A SSID « EmployeesX » (X = Stellan POD Number) dedicated to the company employees. 
- A SSID « GuestsX » (X = Stellan POD Number) dedicated to the guests/visitors. 
 
Once connected to the network, the guests/visitors must have different and restricted access rights than 
employees; therefore, they must be moved to a dedicated VLAN (that we have created in a previous 
exercise).  
 
All this configuration will be performed on the Stellar AP1321 and the OmniSwitch 6360.   
 
 
 
 2 
Creating the Employees SSID 
2.1. 
Connect to the Stellar AP1321  
 
Raspberry Pi « WifiClientX »  
Enter mywifi.al-
enterprise.com:8080 
in the URL bar 
 
Press Enter 
 
Select Administrator 
from the drop-down 
menu 
 
Password: superuser 
 
Click on Login 
 
 
 
2.2. 
Creating the Employees SSID

<<<PAGE 222>>>
4 
Procedure: Employee SSID & Guests SSID Creation 
 
 
- Create the « Employees » SSID 
 
Stellar AP1321 Web Interface  
Click on New in the WLAN window 
 
In the Create a new WLAN window, 
 
- WLAN name: EmployeesX (X = 
Stellan POD number) 
- Security: Personal 
- Password: superuser 
 
Leave the other parameters to their 
default values 
 
Click on Advanced 
 
  
 
Enter VLAN ID: 20 
 
Click on Save 
 
2.2.1. 
Verification 
 
 
Check that the « Employees » SSID has been created correctly 
 
Result 
The Employees SSID (EmployeesX) is created. It appears on the WLAN section:

3.2. 
Managing the Captive Portal 
 
 
- Configure the Captive Portal settings 
- Create the guest account « Guest » 
 
The internal captive portal is activated by default. Now the authentication type must be selected 
(account, access code, or terms of use):  
 
OmniAccess Stellar AP1321 Web Interface    
Open the Access menu 
 
Click on Authentication  
 
 
 
Select the authentication mode 
(here: Account) 
 
- If the authentication mode account 
is selected, an account must be 
created 
- If the authentication mode access 
code is selected, an access code must 
be created  
- If the authentication mode Terms of 
use is selected, the guest access 
attempt will be authorized if he 
accepts a terms of use (customizable) 
 
Click on Add and fill the information 
UserName: Guest 
Password: superuser 
 
The username and password fields 
are case sensitive (ex. The username 
“Guest” is different than “guest”) 
 
Fill the Starting Date and Ending Date 
(validity period account). 
 
Finally, click on Save

WifiClientX

 icon 
(top right)

Stellar Number) 
Check under “More Networks” 
if it is not displayed. 
 
Click on Connect

Conclusion 
In this exercise, we have created 2 SSID:

Procedure: Employee SSID & Guests SSID Creation 
 
-ANNEXES- 
 5 
ANNEX: Configuring the AP as DHCP Server 
5.1. 
Configuring the Employees SSID Network Settings 
 
 
Configure the network settings to make the employees be automatically moved to a 
dedicated subnetwork.  
 
Once the Employees SSID created, the IP settings must now be configured. The aim is to automatically put 
the employee who connects to the « EmployeesX » SSID in a dedicated subnetwork. For employees, we 
will use the 192.168.10.0/24 subnetwork. 
 
Stellar AP1321 Web Browser 
Click on AP in the “AP” window 
 
 
Then, click on the AP IP 
address 
 
 
A new tab opens in the web 
browser  
 
Click on the Network menu 
then AP Networks 
Click on the Manage 
 
button in the vlan10 line 
 
Fill the following IP settings for 
the vlan10:  
- IP Address: 192.168.10.3 
- Subnetwork: 255.255.255.0 
- DNS: 192.168.10.3 
 
Click on Save

<<<PAGE 230>>>
12 
Procedure: Employee SSID & Guests SSID Creation 
 
5.2. 
Configuring the Internal DHCP Server 
 
 
- Activate the internal DHCP server on the OmniAccess Stellar AP1321 
- Create a range dedicated to employees 
 
To allow the employees who connect to the EmployeesX SSID to obtain an IP address, we are going to 
activate the DHCP server embedded on the AP1321:  
 
Stellar AP1321 Web Administration Interface   
Still in the recently opened tab 
 
Click on the Service menu 
 
Click on Create in the DHCP 
tab 
Fill the following settings in 
the new DHCP range:  
- Pool name: Employees 
- Subnet: 255.255.255.0 
- Gateway: 192.168.10.3 
- Range Start: 192.168.10.10 
- Range Stop: 192.168.10.50 
- DNS1: 192.168.10.3  
- Leave the other settings to 
their default values 
 
Click on Save 
 
 
 
Notes 
The DHCP range contains 40 IP addresses. So only 40 devices can be connected simultaneously. If a larger 
amount of employee devices is planned, it is of course possible to increase this amount.  
 
The DHCP range dedicated to employees is now created. It is then necessary to assign it to the network 
“vlan10”. This means that an employee in the VLAN 10 (Employees VLAN) will get its IP address in this 
range. 
 
Select Action in the drop-
down menu 
 
Select Bind Network

<<<PAGE 231>>>
13 
Procedure: Employee SSID & Guests SSID Creation 
 
In the window Bind 
Configuration  
 
Select Bind Network: vlan10  
 
Click on Save 
 
5.3. 
Configuring the Guests SSID Network Settings 
 
 
Configure the Guests SSID network settings 
 
Once the Guests SSID is created and the Captive Portal configured, the network settings must now be

STP REMINDER
• Goal
• Self-configuring algorithm that
maintains a loopfree topology
on a network
• Provides helps to provide data 
path redundancy and network 
scalability
• How it works 
• Supports two Spanning Tree operating modes:
• flat (single STP instance per switch)
• per-VLAN (single STP instance per VLAN)
(By default on OmniSwitch)
• Supports three Spanning Tree operating protocols:
• STP (802.1d): Convergence time :  50 secs
• RSTP (802.1w): Convergence time : < 1 sec
• MSTP (802.1s): < 1 sec

LINK AGGREGATION GROUPS
OMNISWITCH R8
The Alcatel-Lucent name and logo are trademarks of Nokia used under license by ALE.

<<<PAGE 250>>>
Upon completion this module, 
you will be able to:
OBJECTIVES
• Understand the Link Aggregation operation on 
AOS based switches
• Learn how to configure
• Static Link Aggregation
• Dynamic Link Aggregation
• Load Balancing Control

<<<PAGE 251>>>
OVERVIEW
• Goal
• Method of aggregating (combining) more than 2 ports/links so that the switch will “see” them as 
one logical link
• Advantages of Link Aggregation
• Scalability
• Reliability
• Ease of Migration
• Provides an aggregated link
(multiple physical links combined into one logical link)
Logical Link can be statically assigned to any VLAN
802.1q can be configured on the logical aggregated link

<<<PAGE 252>>>
STATIC VS. DYNAMIC
• Difference between Static and Dynamic
• Static
• Port parameters MUST be exactly the same at both ends and within the group
• same speed (e.g., all 10 Mbps, all 100 Mbps, all 1 Gigabit, or all 10 Gigabit)
• Only works between Alcatel-Lucent OmniSwitches
• Dynamic
• IEEE 802.3ad LACP
• LACP will negotiate the optimal parameters for both ends using LACPDU (Link Aggregation Control Protocol 
Data Unit)
• Ports must be of the same speed within the same aggregate group 
• It also works between two different devices such as switches, servers and storage systems.
• Refer to specification guide for the characteristics

<<<PAGE 253>>>
STATIC LINK AGGREGATION GROUPS - CLI
• Creating a Static Aggregate Group
• Adding Ports to a Static Aggregate Group
• Removing Ports from a Static Aggregate Group
-> linkagg static agg <agg_num> size <size> admin-state enable
-> no linkagg static port <Chassis/slot/port>
-> linkagg static port < Chassis/slot/port> agg <agg_num>

<<<PAGE 254>>>
DYNAMIC LINK AGGREGATION GROUPS - CLI
• Configuring a Dynamic Link Aggregation Group
• Assigning ports to the Dynamic Link Aggregation Group
-> linkagg lacp agg <agg_num> size <size> admin-state enable
-> linkagg lacp agg <agg_num>
actor admin-key <actor_admin_key>
-> linkagg lacp port <chassis/slot/port> actor admin-key <actor_admin_key>

<<<PAGE 255>>>
MONITORING
• Static & Dynamic Link Aggregation Groups can be used for VLAN tagging (802.1q)
• Useful monitoring commands:
-> vlan <vlan_id> members linkagg <agg_num> untagged
-> vlan <vlan_id> members linkagg <agg_num> tagged
-> show linkagg

<<<PAGE 257>>>
LINK AGGREGATION STATISTICS
• To display the statistics for a linkagg, all the physical ports in the linkagg are identified, 
and relevant statistics are aggregated and displayed for various show commands. 
Command
Usage 
show linkagg counters
Displays statistics collected for the type and number of packets 
transmitted and received on link aggregate ports.
show linkagg traffic
Displays the total number of packets and bytes that are received and 
transmitted on link aggregate ports.
show linkagg accounting
Displays statistics collected for packets transmitted and received on 
link aggregate ports.
show linkagg port 
Displays information about link aggregation ports.

<<<PAGE 258>>>
LOAD BALANCING CONTROL

<<<PAGE 259>>>
HASHING CONTROL ALGORITHM
• Hashing Control
• Control over the hashing mode
• Link Aggregation
• ECMP
• Server Load Balancing
• Two hashing algorithms available
• Brief Mode 
• UDP/TCP ports not included
• Only Source IP and destination IP addresses 
are considered
• Extended 
• UDP/TCP ports to be included in the hashing 
algorithm
• Result in more efficient load balancing

<<<PAGE 260>>>
LOAD BALANCING MULTICAST ON LINK AGGREGATION 
GROUPS
• Multicast traffic is by default forwarded through the primary port of the Link Aggregation 
Group
• User has the option to enable hashing for non-unicast traffic, which will load balance the 
non-unicast traffic across all ports in the Link Aggregation Group
• If non-ucast option is not specified, link aggregation will only load balance unicast packets

Multi-Switch & Multi-AP Environment 
 
Implementation 
 1 
Initial Topology (OmniSwitch 2360 & AP Stellar 1301) 
In this lab, we will learn how to deploy each of the ALE network devices in a multi-switch / multi-AP 
environment. We will begin by configuring the OmniSwitch 2360 and the Stellar AP1301. We will then connect 
these devices with the OmniSwitch 6360 and the AP Stellar 1321, configured in the previous labs, via the 
backbone (composed of an OS6860, which is connected to both OS6360 and OS2360). 
 
 
Configure the OmniSwitch 2360 and the Stellar AP1301. 
 
 
 2 
Configuring the OmniSwitch 2360 
2.1. 
Logging into the OmniSwitch 2360 
By default, the configuration of the OmniSwitch 2360 is done only through the console port. For security 
reasons, the other protocols (Telnet, SSH, Web interface) are disabled by default. 
 
 
Connect to the OmniSwitch 2360 via the console port with the default credentials 
 
Once OS2360 booted up, use the OS-2360 shortcut to connect through the console port: 
 
R-Lab Remote Desktop 
Double click on the OS-
2360 shortcut

- login: admin 
- password: switch 
 
 
2.2. 
Modifying the OmniSwitch 2360 Configuration 
2.2.1. 
Creating & Assigning the VLAN 10 (AP Management) to the “AP port” and to the uplink port 
 
OS2360-XTE210-> vlan 10  
OS2360-XTE210-> vlan 10 members port 1/1/8 tagged 
OS2360-XTE210-> vlan 10 members port 1/1/6 untagged 
2.2.2. 
Enable the (uplink) port on the OmniSwitch 2360 
Enable port 1/1/8 (to connect the switch to the rest of the network > DHCP, ...): 
 
OS2360-XTE210-> interfaces 1/1/8 admin-state enable 
2.2.3. 
Enable the port connecting the OmniSwitch 2360 to the Stellar 1301 AP 
In the default configuration loaded on the OmniSwitch 2360 switch, user ports are disabled. First, activate 
port 1/1/6 of the OmniSwitch switch that is connected to the Stellar 1301 AP. The OmniAccess Stellar 
AP1301 access point will therefore be connected to the OmniSwitch switch and powered by the PoE 
function. 
 
OS2360-XTE210-> interfaces 1/1/6 admin-state enable 
2.3. 
New Topology 
At this stage of the lab, we have configured the OS2360 and connected it to the rest of the network. In 
the next part, we will focus on the configuration of the OmniAccess Stellar AP1301 access point.

 3 
Checking the OmniAccess Stellar AP1301 
3.1. 
Reminder 
At this stage of the lab, we have:  
- The same VLAN on the ports where are connected the AP1301 and AP1321 (VLAN 10 > AP Management 
VLAN) 
- We did not modify the access point’s Group ID.  
 
As explained in the presentation, if several OmniAccess Stellar access point can reach each other (same 
VLAN, same IP subnetwork) and have the same Group ID, they will automatically form a group.  
 
We can verify that by connecting to the “AdminX” SSID (reminder: the AdminX SSID network is the SSID 
created on the AP1321) from the WifiClientX VM (X=Stellan POD Number):

5 
Multi-Switch & Multi-AP Environment 
 
Make sure that 
Administrator is 
selected 
 
Passphrase: 
superuser 
 
Click on Connect 
 
Result 
You can see that the 2 access points now appear in the “AP” section, meaning that a group has been 
automatically formed, with these 2 access points in it: 
 
 
 
The OmniAccess Stellar AP1301 and AP1321 have automatically regrouped in the same group (AP Group). 
 
The benefits of regrouping APs in the same group are multiple:  
- Simplified management: a unique web interface allows you to manage all APs belonging to the same AP 
Group. You can observe this on your lab; the 2 APs now appear in the “AP” menu: 
 
 
 
- Automatic discovery and integration of OmniAccess Stellar access points belonging to the same AP 
Group as you have observed on your system. 
- Once the access points in the same group, they share information to optimize their configuration as 
much as possible (eg. adaptation of the transmission power to avoid disturbing the transmission of 
other access points). 
 
 
Warning 
WHEN AN OMNIACCESS STELLAR ACCESS POINT GETS IN AN AP GROUP, ITS CONFIGURATION IS DELETED AND 
REPLACED BY THE CONFIGURATION SENT FROM THE PRIMARY VIRTUAL MANAGER (PVM) ACCESS POINT.

<<<PAGE 310>>>
ACTIVATION STATUS - DETAILS
Activation Status
Description
Waiting for first contact
Device has been validated. OV Cirrus 10 is waiting for the device to connect to it. Once the user connects to the network, the 
device will be ready for limited (unlicensed device) or full management (licensed device).
Obtaining Certificate
Device has contacted the OmniVista Cirrus 10 server, and the server is creating a digital certificate that is used in creating the 
secure VPN channel between your device and the OmniVista Cirrus 10 server.
Certificate Previously Issued
A certificate was previously provided to this network device, but it has contacted OmniVista Cirrus 10 without it.
Failed to get certificate
Device has contacted the OmniVista Cirrus 10 Server and the server attempted to create a certificate to achieve a more secure 
communication, but the attempt has failed.
Registered 
Device has successfully registered with OmniVista Cirrus 10 and is ready for limited management.
Upgrade
Device software version will be or is being upgraded.
Upgrading
Device is in the process of upgrading its software.
Upgrade Failed
Device software version upgrade failed.
Assigned
Device has contacted OmniVista Cirrus 10 and was found to be assigned to your account. The device is in the process of becoming 
fully managed.
VPN Configuring
Device is in the process of configuring a secure transport channel for connecting to OmniVista Cirrus 10.
Factory Reset required
The VPN profile was changed/updated. A Factory Reset is required on the device.
Connected to OV
Device has connected to OmniVista Cirrus 10 over a secure transport channel and will proceed with provisioning, if required.
Provisioning
Device is in the process of receiving configuration information from the Provisioning application, before ready for full mgmt.
Provisioning Failed
Device was unable to process the provisioning configuration sent by the Provisioning application, or OmniVista Cirrus 10 was unable 
to discover the device.
OV Managed
Device is ready for full management.
Unsupported Device Model
OmniVista Cirrus 10 does not support the device.

ACTIVATION PROCESS - CLI
AOS
WAN
OmniVista
Cirrus 10
cloud-agent admin-state enable/disable
Enables or disables OV Cirrus functionality for the switch
(default= enable)
cloud-agent admin-state disable force
Reinitialize the VPN between OV Cirrus and the switch
cloud-agent discovery-interval 
Configure the time interval after which the switch will 
call-home the activation server, in case of any error
(default= 30mns)
show cloud-agent status
Displays the Cloud Agent status and parameters 
received from the DHCP and activation server
show cloud-agent vpn status
Displays the Cloud Agent VPN status

Lab: Device On-Boarding on the OmniVista Cirrus 
 
Implementation 
 1 
Introduction 
In this lab, we are going to register the device OmniSwitch 6360 on the OmniVista Cirrus. We can onboard in 
OVC only switches with AOS 8.9R1 or higher so we can’t onboard OmniSwitch 2360 AOS 5.2. 
1.1. 
Topology 
 
 
 
- The OmniSwitch 2360 and 6360 will be assigned a specific “SW-Management” VLAN (VLAN 1305) 
- When an OmniSwitch switch is connected to the network, it automatically tries to reach and register to 
the OmniVista Cirrus Instance.

<<<PAGE 338>>>
4 
Lab: Device On-Boarding on the OmniVista Cirrus 
 
 2 
Configuring the OmniSwitch 2360 & OmniSwitch 6360  
 
 
Perform the necessary configuration on the OS6360 to be able to reach the OmniVista 
Cirrus. 
2.1. 
Checking the “cloudagent” File Presence 
The URL of the OmniVista Cirrus instance that the switch must join and register to is stored in a 
cloudagent.cfg file.  
- Check the presence of the “cloudagent.cfg” file on the switche OS6360:  
 
-> ls /flash/working 
Nos.img         cloudagent.cfg  vcboot.cfg 
Uos.img         pkg             vcsetup.cfg 
- Check the content of the cloudagent.cfg file :  
 
-> more /flash/working/cloudagent.cfg 
Activation Server URL: eu.activation.ovng.myovcloud.com 
HTTP Proxy Server: 
HTTP Proxy Port: 
HTTP Proxy User Name: 
 
 
Warning 
IF THE FILE IS NOT PRESENT, TYPE THE FOLLOWING COMMAND TO COPY IT FROM A BACKUP DIRECTORY 
(“CIRRUS” DIRECTORY):  
 
-> cp /flash/cirrus/cloudagent.cfg /flash/working/cloudagent.cfg 
2.2. 
Link the switches to the network core 
To ease the management, we are going to create a VLAN dedicated for switch management purposes 
(VLAN 1305) and assign it to the ports as described in the diagram below.  
We will then create a static/default route on each switch to forward traffic (ex. Internet) to a router 
located in the backbone area.  
- Topology at the end of this step:  
 
 
 
               
        
                 
                  
     
     
     
     
     
     
      
      
STELLAR POD  (    POD N M ER)

<<<PAGE 339>>>
5 
Lab: Device On-Boarding on the OmniVista Cirrus 
 
On the OmniSwitch 6360:  
 
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 1305 name SW-MANAGEMENT  
OS6360-XTE210-> ip interface “int_sw-mgmt” address 10.130.5.5/24 vlan 1305    
OS6360-XTE210-> vlan 1305 members port 1/1/3 tagged 
OS6360-XTE210-> ip static-route 0.0.0.0/0 gateway 10.130.5.7 
- On the OS2360:  
 
OS2360-XTE210-> vlan 1305 name SW-MANAGEMENT  
OS2360-XTE210-> ip interface “int_sw_mgmt” address 10.130.5.4/24 vlan 1305 
OS2360-XTE210-> vlan 1305 members port 1/1/8 tagged 
OS2360-XTE210-> ip static-route 0.0.0.0/0 gateway 10.130.5.7 
2.3. 
Configuring the Services  
2.3.1. 
SNMP 
Switches that connect to the OmniVista Cirrus exchange SNMP packets. Therefore, the SNMP traffic must 
be authorized. Enter the following commands on switches OS6360: 
 
-> aaa authentication default local   
-> snmp security authentication all  
2.3.2. 
DNS & NTP 
The DNS and NTP services must be activated on the switches: 
- A DNS address must be configured to access the OmniVista Cirrus URL 
- The NTP client must be activated to synchronize the switches date and hour to the OmniVista Cirrus 
date and hour. 
 
Configure the DNS and NTP on both switches (OS6360 AND OS2360): 
-> ntp client admin-state enable 
-> ip domain-name remote-lab.com 
-> ip name-server 9.9.9.9 
-> ip domain-lookup 
2.3.3. 
Enable the “cloud-agent” service 
- To finish, to connect to the OmniVista Cirrus, the cloud-agent service must be enabled on switch 
OS6360: 
 
-> cloud-agent admin-state enable 
The switches are now configured, and ready to be registered in the OmniVista Cirrus. In the next part, we 
are going to learn how to declare and register the 6360 from the OmniVista Cirrus web interface.

5.2. 
OmniSwitch activation 
- In the Device Catalog, scroll horizontally to find the column “Activation Status” ( se the expand/collapse 
button 
 if you can’t scroll): 
The activation Status of the OmniSwitch is “Waiting for validation” during a short period. OmniVista Cirrus 
automatically validates the device. 
The activation status of the switches is then “Waiting for first Contact”.  
OmniVista Cirrus is waiting for the device to connect to it. 
 
Network devices automatically contact the OmniVista Cirrus Server at regular “call home” intervals. For 
these devices, there is no need to manually start or restart the activation process. The devices will 
automatically contact the OmniVista Cirrus 10 Server at the configured Call Home interval and complete 
the activation process. 
The Activation process is performed automatically on the OmniSwitch. But the call home interval might 
take too much time. You can force the call home with one of the following methods of your choice: 
1. Restart the activation process manually (recommended method): 
OS6360 
-> cloud-agent admin-state disable force 
-> cloud-agent admin-state enable 
2. Or reboot the devices as follow: 
OS6360 
-> reload from working no rollback-timeout 
For both cases, the Device Activation Status should display “Connected to OV”, followed by “Provisioning” 
and "OV Managed", meaning the device is successfully registered and ready for full management with the 
OmniVista Cirrus 10 Server. This process should take about 2 minutes.

Management” VLAN (VLAN 10).

4.1.1.

4.2. 
Troubleshooting the Stellar AP  
4.2.1. 
Resetting the Stellar AP 
A good way to start the troubleshooting of a Stellar AP is to ensure that it has been reset to its factory 
settings.  
- If you can access to the Stellar AP: 
 
Stellar AP 
  > Plug the AP to a PoE port 
  > Press 6 seconds on the Reset button available at the rear of the AP (until the led blinks red) 
 
- If you can’t access to the Stellar AP, but have access to its Serial port:

11 
Lab: Stellar Access Point Provisioning 
 
PC 
  > Open a serial connection (via a software as Putty, Teraterm…) 
    > Baud rate: 115200

    > Parity: None 
    > Stop bits: 1 
 
  > login: support 
  > password: aos2016 
 
Reset the Stellar AP to its factory settings 
support@AP-0E:E0:~$ ssudo firstboot 
This will erase all settings and remove any installed packages. Are you sure [N/y]? y

4.2.2. 
Checking the Stellar AP Mode (OV/Cluster/OVNG) 
To register to the OmniVista Cirrus, the Stellar AP must run in OV mode: 
 
Stellar Serial Console (logged as support) 
support@AP-0E:E0:~$ getmode 
OVNG 
 
4.2.3. 
Checking the Stellar AP DHCP Mode (DHCP/Static) 
The DHCP Server sends the OmniVista IP address to the Stellar AP via a specific option (138/43). To ensure 
that the Stellar AP is in DHCP mode:  
 
Stellar Serial Console (logged as support) 
support@AP-0E:E0:~$ cat /etc/config/network 
 
config interface 'loopback' 
        option ifname 'lo' 
        option proto 'static' 
        option ipaddr '127.0.0.1' 
        option netmask '255.0.0.0' 
 
config globals 'globals' 
        option ula_prefix 'fd66:ce37:fd0b::/48' 
 
config interface 'wan' 
        option ifname 'eth0' 
        option type 'bridge' 
        option proto 'dhcp' 
        option force_link '1'

Lab: Employee SSID Creation 
 
Implementation 
 1 
Objectives 
Now that the devices in the Remote Lab have been discovered in OmniVista Cirrus 10, let’s create multiple 
SSIDs (employee, guest…). In this first lab, we will focus on how to create a secured Employee SSID. 
1.1. 
POD Topology 
 
 
 
 
Steps to create an Employee SSID in the Remote Lab: 
1. Create VLAN 20. This VLAN will service the SSID “EmployeeX” (X = R-Lab Number). It will be tagged 
from the Access Points to the Access OmniSwitches (2360 and 6360), and over the link to the 
OmniSwitch 6870.  
2. Create the SSID and configure its options.

<<<PAGE 390>>>
4 
Lab: Employee SSID Creation 
 
 2 
Creation of the Employeee VLAN & IP Interface 
Before creating the Employee SSID, let’s create the VLAN and IP interface that will be associated to this SSID 
EmployeeX (X= R-Lab number) and that will carry the employee traffic.  
2.1. 
Employee VLAN 
 
Create the VLAN 20 on the OmniSwitches 6360 and 2360. 
 
 
 
 
- The VLAN application of Omnivista Cirrus will be used to create and manage the Employee VLAN 
- Go to LAN > Layer 2 > VLAN.  
- Click on + Create VLAN. 
 
- In the Create VLAN page, configure the following: 
o 
VLAN IDs: 20 
o 
VLAN(s) Admin Status: Enabled 
o 
VLAN Description: Employee 
 
- In the Switch Selection section, select the OS-6360A 
 
- In the Selected Devices section, click on the Add Ports link in the Q Tagged Ports column for the OS6360-
A: 
o 
In the Port Selection window, choose ports 1/1/3 and 1/1/6 and move them to the right section 
of the window using the > button. Click Ok.

<<<PAGE 391>>>
5 
Lab: Employee SSID Creation 
 
 
 
- Click Create. 
- Employee VLAN 20 now appears in the VLANs list (use the refresh button if it is not): 
 
 
 
- As the OS2360 is not managed by Omnivista Cirrus, we are going to open a Console session to create the 
VLAN manually. Double-click on the OS-2360 console icon and type the following commands: 
 
 
 
vlan 20  
vlan 20 name EMPLOYEE 
vlan 20 members port 1/1/6 tagged 
vlan 20 members port 1/1/8 tagged

<<<PAGE 392>>>
6 
Lab: Employee SSID Creation 
 
 3 
Employee SSID Creation 
Now that we have the Employee VLAN and the associated IP interface, let’s create the Employee SSID: 
 
 
3.1. 
Employee SSID Creation 
 
 
Create the SSID EmployeesX (X = R-Lab Number) 
 
> Select Wireless > SSIDs 
  > Click on the + Create SSID button 
 > In the Basic Information section complete the following: 
    > Profile Name: EmployeesX (X = R-Lab number) 
    > SSID: <filled automatically> 
    > Usage: Enterprise Network for Employees (802.1X) 
 
 
    
 
Notes > About the “Usage” 
During the SSID creation, a “Usage” is asked. When you select a Usage, relevant related default configurations 
(Access Policy, Authentication Strategy, …) are automatically created.  
Of course, these configurations can be customized. Check the Omnivista Cirrus dedicated Help for more 
information.  
 
  > Encryption Type: WPA2_AES  
  > Allowed Band: 2.4GHz and 5GHz 
   
 
Tips > Help Menu 
As you can see, several settings can be managed in the SSID Creation properties. Take the time to learn more 
about each of them by clicking on the Help button   
 
 
Notes > UPAMRadiusServer 
In this lab, for all the types of authentication, we will use the UPAM platform (Unified Policy Authentication 
Manager) embedded in OmniVista Cirrus 10.  
UPAM is a unified access management platform for both AOS Switch Series devices and Stellar AP Series 
devices. UPAM supports both captive portal server and RADIUS server; and can be used to implement multiple 
authentication methods, such as MAC authentication, 802.1X authentication, and captive portal authentication. 
 
> In the Authentication Strategy section complete the following:   
  > RADIUS Server: UPAMRadiusServer 
  > Click on Manage Employee Accounts 
  > Select Launch ‘Manage Employee Accounts’ page in a new browser tab

<<<PAGE 393>>>
7 
Lab: Employee SSID Creation 
 
3.2. 
Employee Account Creation 
 
 
Create the Employee account 
 
> Click on the + Create Employee Account button 
  > Username: Employee  
  > Password: password 
  > Repeat Password: password 
  > Do not modify other attributes 
  > Click on Create 
 
 
Tips > Importing Employee Accounts Information 
You can automatically import a csv/xlsx file containing Employee Account information 
by clicking on the Import button at the top of the screen.    
3.3. 
Back to… Creating the EmployeesX SSID 
 
Return to the Create SSID window 
In the Authentication Strategy > Access Policy section configure the following: 
  > Select Configure Access Policy 
  > Leave the Policy Name to its assigned name. 
  > Set the Authentication Source option to Local Database 
  > Set the Web Authentication option to None 
 
 
Web Authentication 
For Employee networks, typically a Captive Portal is not required as a different type of 
authentication is used (802.1x in this example).   
 
In the Default VLAN/Network section configure the following: 
  > Select Configure Access Role Attributes 
  > Leave the Profile Name to its assigned name  
 
- For now, we are leaving all the other additional SSID settings to their default values. 
- Click Next.

<<<PAGE 394>>>
8 
Lab: Employee SSID Creation 
 
3.4. 
Assigning the SSID to the AP Group 
 
 
Assign the SSID EmployeesX to the AP Group created in the previous lab 
 
Now that the SSID EmployeeX has been created, it has to be assigned to one or multiple AP Group(s): 
 
In the Network Assignments section 
> Click on + Add Site button 
  > In the Select Site to filter groups option, select your Site “My Site” 
  > In the Select Access Points Groups option, select My-AP-Group 
  > Also, Remove default device group from the list 
> Click Next 
 
In the Schedule and VLAN mappings section 
> Click on Edit button that is in the VLAN/Tunnel Mapping column and on your “My-AP-Group” line. 
  > In the VLAN/Tunnel Mapping window, set the Choose Network Mapping to VLAN 
  > Set the VLAN ID to 20 
  > Click Apply 
> Click Create to complete the SSID creation. 
 
 
Setting a Schedule 
By default, the availability schedule for AP Groups is set to "Always Available". However, you can schedule 
availability for specific times/days of the week. You can set the same availability schedule for all selected AP 
Groups, or set different schedules for each group.

 5 
Monitoring the Connections 
 
Display the EmployeesX authentication record  
5.1. 
UPAM Monitoring 
The UPAM platform (Unified Policy Authentication Manager) is embedded in the OmniVista Cirrus 10. This 
module is used to implement authentication (MAC authentication, 802.1x, Captive Portal…) 
The Authentication Record Screen displays authentication information for all devices authenticated 
through UPAM:  
 
> Select Network > Access Records > Authentication Records 
 
 
 
 
 
In the Authentication Record List information, find the Stellar Access Point where your 
Client is connected.   
 
 
Client associated to a Stellar Access Point 
The information asked in the question above can also be found in the Network > Analytics > 
Clients menu. Go check it out! 
 
 
Employee Account Creation 
Do you remember the Employee account that you have created? You have done it via a shortcut, during the 
SSID creation process. This shortcut leads to the … Network Access > Accounts > Employee Account menu! Go 
and have a look at this menu. You will find the Employee account that you have created previously. From 
there, you can easily create a new Employee account.

Lab: Guest SSID Creation 
 
Implementation 
 1 
Objectives 
In the previous lab, we have learned how to create an Employee SSID dedicated for company employees. 
Now, let’s see how to create a Guest SSID dedicated for Guest users. 
1.1. 
POD Topology 
 
 
 
Steps to create a Guest SSID in the Remote Lab: 
1. Create VLAN 30. This VLAN will service the SSID “GuestsX” (X = R-Lab Number). It will be tagged from 
the Access Points to the Access OmniSwitches (2360 and 6360), and over the link to the OmniSwitch 
6870.  
2. Create the SSID and configure its options.

<<<PAGE 418>>>
4 
Lab: Guest SSID Creation 
 
 2 
Creation of the Guest VLAN & IP Interface 
Before creating the Guest SSID, let’s create the VLAN and IP interface that will be associated to this SSID 
GuestsX (X= R-Lab number) and that will carry the guest traffic.  
2.1. 
Guest VLAN 
 
Create the VLAN 30 on the OmniSwitches 6360 and 2360. 
 
- The VLAN application of Omnivista Cirrus 10 will be used to create and manage the Employee VLAN 
- Go to LAN > Layer 2 > VLAN.  
- Click on + Create VLAN. 
- In the Create VLAN page, configure the following: 
VLAN IDs: 30 
VLAN(s) Admin Status: Enabled 
VLAN Description: Guests 
- In the Switch Selection section, select the OS-6360A 
 
- In the Selected Devices section, click on the Add Ports link in the Q Tagged Ports column for the OS6360-
A: 
In the Port Selection window, choose ports 1/1/3 and 1/1/6 and move them to the right section of the 
window using the > button. Click Ok. 
- Click Create. 
- Employee VLAN 20 now appears in the VLANs list (use the refresh button if it is not) 
 
- As the OS2360 is not managed by Omnivista Cirrus, we are going to open a Console session to create the 
VLAN manually. Double-click on the OS-2360 console icon and type the following commands: 
 
 
 
vlan 30  
vlan 30 name GUEST 
vlan 30 members port 1/1/6 tagged 
vlan 30 members port 1/1/8 tagged

<<<PAGE 419>>>
5 
Lab: Guest SSID Creation 
 
 3 
Guest SSID Creation 
Now that we have the Guest VLAN and its associated IP interface, let’s create the Guest SSID: 
 
 
3.1. 
Guest SSID Creation 
 
 
Create the SSID GuestsX (X = R-Lab Number) 
 
> Select Wireless > SSIDs 
  > Click on the + Create SSID button 
 > In the Basic Information section complete the following: 
    > Profile Name: GuestsX (X = R-Lab number) 
    > SSID: <filled automatically> 
    > Usage: Guest Network (Open or Captive Portal)  
    > Do you want users to go through a Captive Portal? YES  
    > Captive Portal Type: OV-UPAM Captive Portal 
    > Allowed Band: 2.4GHz and 5GHz 
    > Enhanced Open: Disabled 
 
 
 
    
 
Notes > About the “Usage” 
During the SSID creation, a “Usage” is asked. When you select a Usage, relevant related default configurations 
(Access Policy, Authentication Strategy, …) are automatically created.  
Of course, these configurations can be customized. Check the Omnivista Cirrus dedicated Help for more 
information.  
 
 
Tips > Help Menu 
As you can see, several settings can be managed in the SSID Creation properties. Take the time to learn more 
about each of them by clicking on the Help button   
 
 
Notes > UPAMRadiusServer 
In this lab, for all the types of authentication, we will use the UPAM platform (Unified Policy Authentication 
Manager) embedded in OmniVista Cirrus.  
UPAM is a unified access management platform for both AOS Switch Series devices and Stellar AP Series 
devices. UPAM supports both captive portal server and RADIUS server; and can be used to implement multiple 
authentication methods, such as MAC authentication, 802.1X authentication, and captive portal authentication. 
 
> In the Authentication Strategy section complete the following:   
  > RADIUS Server: UPAMRadiusServer 
  > Click on Manage Guest Accounts 
  > Select Launch ‘Manage Guest Accounts’ page in a new browser tab

<<<PAGE 420>>>
6 
Lab: Guest SSID Creation 
 
3.2. 
Guest Account Creation 
 
 
Create the Guest account 
 
> Click on the + Create Guest Account button 
  > Username: Guest  
  > Password: password 
  > Repeat Password: password 
  > Do not modify other attributes 
  > Click on Create 
 
 
Tips > Importing Guest Accounts Information 
You can automatically import a csv/xlsx file containing Guest Account information 
by clicking on the Import button at the top of the screen.   
3.3. 
Back to… Creating the GuestsX SSID 
 
Return to the Create SSID window 
In the Authentication Strategy > Access Policy section configure the following: 
  > Select Configure Access Policy 
  > Leave the Policy Name to its assigned name. 
  > Set the Allow All EAPs to Yes. 
  > Set the Authentication Source option to None 
  > Set the Web Authentication option to Guest 
 
 
Web Authentication 
For Guest networks, a Captive Portal is required to authenticate the guest users.   
 
  > Click on Create Guest Access Strategy 
  > Select Launch ‘Create Guest Access Strategy’ page in a new browser tab 
  > In the Basic Information section configure the following: 
  > Strategy Name: Guests_OVX (where X is the POD number) 
  > Click on Create Template 
  > Select Launch ‘Create Captive Portal Template’ page in a new browser tab 
 
  > In the Basic Information section configure the following: 
  > Name: My_CPTemplate_X (where X is the POD number) 
  > Layout Information: Select any of the available layout types 
  > Click Create 
 
  > Return to the Guest Access Strategy window 
  > Captive Portal Template: Select My_CPTemplate_X (where X is the POD number) 
  > In the Login Strategy section select: 
  > Login By: Username & Password 
  > Click Create 
 
 
 
Login Strategy 
As you can see In the login Strategy section, there are multiple options for the guest users to 
authenticate: Microsoft Azure and social login using Facebook or Rainbow. For this lab, only the 
UPAM Captive Portal will be used   
 
  > Return to the Create SSID window 
  > Select the Guest Access Strategy Guests_OVX (where X is the POD number)  
 
  > In the Default VLAN/Network section: 
  > Select Configure Access Role Attribute 
  > Leave the Profile Name to its assigned name.

5.1. 
UPAM Monitoring 
The UPAM platform (Unified Policy Authentication Manager) is embedded in the OmniVista Cirrus 10. This 
module is used to implement authentication (MAC authentication, 802.1x, Captive Portal…) 
 
The Authentication Record Screen displays authentication information for all devices authenticated 
through UPAM:  
 
> Select Network > Access Records > Authentication Records 
 
 
 
 
In the Authentication Record List information, find the Stellar Access Point where your 
Client is connected.  Look for the Additional information button in the Actions column 
 
 
Client associated to a Stellar Access Point 
The information asked in the question above can also be found in the Network > Analytics > 
Clients menu. Go check it out! 
 
 
Guest Account Creation 
Do you remember the Guest account that you have created? You have done it via a shortcut, during the SSID 
creation process. This shortcut leads to the … Network Access > Accounts > Guest Account menu! Go and 
have a look at this menu. You will find the Guest account that you have created previously. From there, you 
can easily create a new Guest account.
