Lab: WLAN Stellar AP Operation and Maintenance 
 
Implementation 
 1 
Objectives 
 
With OmniVista Cirrus you can perform different operation and maintenances tasks on the managed devices, 
such as image upgrade on a Stellar Access Point.

<<<PAGE 451>>>
3 
Lab: WLAN Stellar AP Operation and Maintenance 
 
 2 
Schedule upgrade 
 
A dedicated application is available on OmniVista Cirrus to perform Scheduled upgrades of the Stellar 
Access Points. Use this section only as a configuration guide, and do not complete this upgrade process. 
 
 
Warning 
THE STELLAR AP HAVE ALREADY THE DESIRED SOFTWARE VERSION.  
THIS SECTION EXPLAINS ONLY HOW TO SCHEDULE THE UPGRADE OF THE STELLAR FIRMWARE, DO NOT 
COMPLETE THIS SCHEDULE UPGRADE.   
 
- Select the first tab Scheduled Upgrades.  
- Click the button + Create Upgrade schedule. 
- This process is divided into 4 steps. 
- In the Step 1 “Schedule Setting”: 
- Name the schedule: AP-Upgrade 
- Use the Time zone: (UTC +01:00) – Europe/Paris 
- Set the occurrence to : Every “Month”, on the day “1”, at “12:00 PM” 
- Keep the Upgrade Duration at 6 hours. It means that the upgrade of the Stellar APs will occur between 
12:00PM (starting date) and 06:00AM (+6 hours). 
- Set the start date one month after the current date, which means the first upgrade would occur in a 
month. 
 
 
Warning 
AS EXPLAINED, WE ARE NOT PERFORMING THE UPGRADE OF THE STELLAR APS OF THE REMOTE LABS, JUST 
EXPLAIN THE PROCEDURE.  
 
- And the end date in two months from the current date. 
- Click Next.

<<<PAGE 452>>>
4 
Lab: WLAN Stellar AP Operation and Maintenance 
 
- In the Step 2 “AP Groups Selection”: 
- Select your site My Site in the first line, Site selection. This field is mandatory. 
- In the second line “Select Access Points Groups”, select My-AP-Group. We want to upgrade all the 
Stellar APs of this group. 
- Otherwise, if we want to upgrade a specific Stellar AP, select a device in the third line “Select 
Devices”. 
- Click Next. 
 
- In the Step 3 “Set Software Version”: 
- Either check the box “Set same software version for all selected groups” and select the desired 
software version (or the value Do Not Upgrade) for all the devices of all AP Groups. 
- Or uncheck this box and specify in the table below the Desired software version for each AP Group 
selected. 
- Select the latest version and click on Next: 
 
 
 
- In the Step 4 “Review”: 
- Review all the parameters configured in the previous steps. 
- Click on the Cancel button. 
 
 
Warning 
AS EXPLAINED PREVIOUSLY, USE THIS PART OF THE LAB AS A PROCEDURE AND DO NOT CREATE IT. 
OUR REMOTE LABS ARE SET UP WITH THE APPROPRIATE SOFTWARE VERSION FOR THE STELLAR ACCESS POINTS 
AND WE DO NOT NEED TO UPDATE THEM TO ANY DIFFERENT VERSIONS. THANKS!   
 
Note that when a device is upgraded, it will reboot with the new image. It will then become unavailable 
during this upgrade duration and all the end clients connected to this device will be disconnected.

<<<PAGE 453>>>
5 
Lab: WLAN Stellar AP Operation and Maintenance 
 
 3 
Diagnostic Tools 
The following applications are provided to assist you with analyzing and troubleshooting your network: 
Network Events - Monitor notification events and configure event management tasks. 
Collect Support Information - Collect log files from a network device that you can send to Alcatel-Lucent 
Enterprise (ALE) to troubleshoot problems. 
3.1. 
Network Events 
 
- Go to Diagnostic Tools > Network Events 
- Make sure that the Site that was created previously which includes all managed devices is selected at the 
top right side of the screen.  
- Also, you can define a specific time period to display more entries in the webpage.  
- Select Events from the top menu. 
- You can navigate between AP Traps, Switch Traps and QoE Analytics to show all the generated events. 
 
 
 
AP Traps 
Notifications events form the Access Points  
 
 
QoE Analytics 
Events from devices that are sent to OmniVista Cirrus R10 for monitoring and analysis 
 
 
Switch Traps 
Notifications events form the OmniSwitches 
 
3.2. 
Collect Support Information 
 
- Go to Diagnostic Tools > Collect Support Info 
- Click on the Start Collection of Support info button. 
 
 
Collect Support Information 
Collects log information from network devices that you can send to the Alcatel-Lucent Enterprise (ALE) team to 
troubleshoot problems. The log files can be downloaded to a compressed file (tar.gz for Access Point devices 
and Switches). 
 
- Select any of the available Access Points and click on Start Collection Now. 
- You should see an Uploading File Message in the Status column. 
- Wait a minute and then click on Refresh: 
 
 
- The message in the Status column changes to Collected and a File name is assigned to this new file. 
- You can then download the file using the Download button:

<<<PAGE 454>>>
6 
Lab: WLAN Stellar AP Operation and Maintenance 
 
 
 
- Click again on the Start Collection of Support info button. 
- Select any of the available OmniSwitches and click Next. 
- Different options can be defined as follows: 
 
 
 
swlog files  
Collects all switch log files  
 
 
cfg files 
Collects configuration files (e.g. vcboot.cfg) from the Certified and Working Directories 
 
 
Tech Support 
Includes all Configuration, Switch Log Files and Tech Support log file information like device hardware and 
firmware information 
 
 
Tech Support layer 2 
Includes all Configuration, Switch Log Files and Layer 2 log file information such as Layer 2 interface 
information.  
 
 
Tech Support layer 3 
Includes all Configuration, Switch Log Files and Layer 3 log file information such as Layer 3 IP information. 
 
 
Tech Support Engineering Complete 
Includes all Configuration, Switch Log Files and Engineering log file information (Virtual Chassis information). 
- Click on Start Collecting Now. 
- You should get the Uploading File status in the Status column. 
- Wait for about a minute and then click on Refresh. 
 
- The message in the Status column changes to Collected and the File name assigned. 
- You can then download the file using the Download button. 
 4 
Alerts  
OmniVista Cirrus can report alerts received from network devices. 
- Go to Alerts 
- Make sure that the Site that was created previously which includes all managed devices is selected at the 
top right side of the screen.  
- Also, you can define a larger time period (Last 7 days) to display more entries in the webpage.  
 
 
The top section of the Alerts screen provides a summary of alert activity during the 
specified time range

<<<PAGE 455>>>
7 
Lab: WLAN Stellar AP Operation and Maintenance 
 
- Look for the most recent entries in the Alerts Entry List 
 
 
 The bottom section of the screen is the Alerts Entry List, which displays the 
alerts received from network devices within the specified time range. 
 
 
 
 
New Alerts 
New Alerts are displayed and updated in real-time. New Alerts received are also indicated when a red blinking 
dot appears to the top of the bell icon on the Organization banner

<<<PAGE 456>>>
8 
Lab: WLAN Stellar AP Operation and Maintenance 
 
 5 
Device Troubleshooting 
The Device Troubleshooting application is used to send commands to network devices to troubleshoot and 
resolve device problems. A list of commands is provided. You can use these commands as is or edit them for 
your specific situation.  
 
- Go to Diagnostic Tools > Device Troubleshooting 
- Click on the + Assign Command button. 
- Select any of the available Access Points in the Device Selection window. 
- Click Next. 
- You can see a list of the available commands in the Command Selection window. 
- Select the setDateTime command and click on the > arrow to move it to the right side of the screen. 
- Click Next. 
- You can modify the date and time accordingly if needed in the Command Argument Editing window. 
- Click Next. 
- Click Assign Command. 
 
- Wait for a few minutes and then look for the result of the command. 
- You may have to click on the Full Screen button to display it. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
You may test the different commands available and see which arguments are 
required for each command.

OMNISWITCH LIGHTNING CONFIG INTRO
• Benefits of OmniSwitch Lightning Config
• Easy equipment deployment with minimal expertise
• Reduced chances of errors
• Enhanced security through local authentication and HTTPS
• Flexibility to configure switches to specific needs
Port 1
DHCP Client
Default IP interface VLAN1
192.168.0.1

<<<PAGE 474>>>
OMNISWITCH LIGHTNING CONFIG INTRO
The Power of OmniSwitch Lightning Config
1.Boot and Login:
•
No configuration required on the laptop
•
Only need an Ethernet cable, web browser, and basic networking knowledge
•
No special apps or adapters needed
2.Apply Template:
•
Supports major browsers (iPhone, Android, Windows, Mac)
•
No need for static IP on remote devices
•
Automation minimizes human errors
3.Go! (No reboot required):
•
Seamless template creation and deployment
•
Export templates for other switches

<<<PAGE 475>>>
LIGHTNING CONFIG OVERVIEW
ALE Lightning Config simplifies small to medium 
networks
• Fast Setup: Go from unboxing to passing traffic in less 
than 5 minutes per switch.
• Minimal Training: Learn to use Lightning Config in under 
15 minutes.
• Works with Any ALE 6360 Switch: Compatible for PoE, 
link aggregation, and multicast configurations.

<<<PAGE 476>>>
ESSENTIAL PREPARATION FOR LIGHTNING CONFIG
Check List:
5.Browser and Access: Use a supported web 
browser (Chrome, Safari) on Windows, 
Mac, iPhone, or Android.
6.Make sure HTTPS access is enabled for a 
secure connection.
7.Templates:
If applying a template, have it ready and 
ensure it is correctly configured for your 
network.
1.IP Addresses:
Ensure you have the IP address, default 
gateway, and subnet mask ready for your 
network.
2.Login Credentials:
Confirm the admin username and password
for the switch.
3.Ethernet Connection:Have a working 
Ethernet cable to connect your laptop to the 
switch.
4.Ensure your laptop’s network settings are 
set to DHCP (obtain IP address 
automatically).

<<<PAGE 477>>>
LIGHTNING CONFIG: PREPARE FOR INSTALL
• If you don’t understand IP addressing, Subnet Masks, and/or 
Default Gateways please stop and contact the solution 
architect that designed the network
• Do not pre-cable the ALE switch to the network.
• Do not connect the ALE switch to any other switch.
• Do not connect cameras or other devices to the switch before 
setup.
• Wait to power up the ALE switch until the laptop is ready.
• Do not connect the ALE switch to a DHCP server.
READ THIS FIRST
Proper connection from switch to laptop
Before Starting:
• Configure the laptop for DHCP client mode.
• Use an Ethernet cable to connect the laptop to port 1 of the 
switch.
• Ensure there’s only one Ethernet connection between the 
laptop and switch.

For Windows:

select Properties.

6.Ensure Obtain an IP address automatically and 
Obtain DNS server address automatically are
selected.
7.Click OK and Close to save the settings.

• You must use https
• Click on Advanced
The laptop will 
be assigned an IP 
of 
192.168.0.200/2
4 via the DHCP 
server on the ALE 
switch.

<<<PAGE 483>>>
LIGHTNING CONFIG: LOGIN STEPS
• Click on Proceed to 192.168.0.1 (unsafe) to bypass 
the warning and access the switch configuration page.
2-Log into the ALE switch
• Username is: admin
• Password is: switch
1-Accept the self signed certificate and log in as admin

<<<PAGE 484>>>
LIGHTNING CONFIG: MINIMUM VIABLE CONFIGURATION
1.Click on RECOMMENDED DEFAULTS
2.Click on LIGHTNING CONFIG
• Do NOT skip the Recommended Defaults!
3.Enter the required information
• Every IP Address must be unique on the same 
network!
• Get help if you are unsure about IP, Subnet, or 
Gateway settings

<<<PAGE 485>>>
LIGHTNING CONFIG: CHANGE THE ADMIN PASSWORD
1. Apply the Configuration
•
After making changes, click YES to apply the 
configuration.
2. Change the Admin Password
•
Do not leave the default password in place. It is not 
secure
•
The new password must meet the following 
requirements:
•
At least 8 characters
•
1 uppercase letter
•
1 lowercase letter
•
1 digit
•
1 special character (but avoid using ! or $).
3. Save the New Password

<<<PAGE 486>>>
LIGHTNING CONFIG: SAVE ALL CHANGES AND CERTIFY
1. Click Yes
•
Leave the toggle button on to save the 
configuration as the working version.
2. Be Patient
Saving can take a few minutes. Check the green success 
message at the bottom once complete.
3. After Saving:
•
Go to the Main Page to continue (see Red arrow):
•
Connect edge devices.
•
Connect other preconfigured ALE switches.
•
Never connect an out-of-box ALE switch to 
another without running Lightning Config first.

<<<PAGE 494>>>
LOOP AVOIDANCE
Networks are intended to all computers, servers, phones, cameras, door locks, etc. to communicate to 
one another.
Physical loops in networks can be very bad for the health of the network as it can cause 
communication from the above devices to continually circle the network and slow down or even halt 
effective communication.
There are networking technologies for loop avoidance
In both sample topologies of this presentation there are physical loops
If you have been given instructions to interconnect switches in a manner similar to those listed in the 
preceding templates, please STOP and consult with the solution architect to ensure they have 
implemented loop avoidance network technologies

<<<PAGE 498>>>
ALE OMNIVISTA SMART TOOL (OST)
Feature overview
• Application for installations and problems resolution
• Quickly identify any device connected to the ALE 
switch
• One button repairs for PoE devices that won’t boot
• Debug and open tickets with Tech Support with a 
single button
• One button network health analysis
• Download and save switch configurations
• Easy switch management functions, reboot, save 
changes, and smart factory resets
• Lighting Config installs for any ALE switch

<<<PAGE 499>>>
ALE OMNIVISTA SMART TOOL (OST)
Power over ethernet wizard
• Verify every PoE device on a switch
• Diagnose and resolve many common PoE problems 
with one click wizard
• Automates TAC’s trouble shooting list
• Check remaining PoE budget
• Warns about any outdated software
• Automatically saves any changes

<<<PAGE 500>>>
ALE OMNIVISTA SMART TOOL (OST)
Features
• Reset Port
• Cable Test
• Ping

<<<PAGE 501>>>
ALE OMNIVISTA SMART TOOL (OST)
Auto-ticket
• Installers can’t wait for a Tech Support call back!
• Enable low level debug
• Automatically resets the port
• Collects all tech support info required
• Copies debug to the installer's laptop
• Builds a template to send to tech support
• Submit ticket and keep doing installs
• Allows TAC to start working on the problem right away 
without requesting more information

<<<PAGE 502>>>
ALE OMNIVISTA SMART TOOL (OST)
Traffic Analysis
• Optional but recommended
• Check the network for common problems
•
Bad cable or SFP
•
Lost packets on uplinks or severs
•
Too many broadcasts
•
Multicast problems
• Save the analysis results for expert review
• Ready for an intelligent data feed to Artificial 
Intelligence
Detailed Analysis Report
AI Review

<<<PAGE 503>>>
ALE OMNIVISTA SMART TOOL (OST)
Config wizard and backup / restore
• Rapid and highly simplified switch configuration like 
Lighting Config on ANY ALE switch.
• No console cables, no CLI, no switch management 
training required

modifying AOS in any way
• Simplified one time switch backups and restore for 
easy RMA swaps or emergency restores
